The former scriptural references are putting their point in a negative way, and the latter references are putting their point in a positive way. Thus we can see that it's the mind which is the one single principal cause that sets all life into motion. The principal cause is not something other than the mind. Therefore when the sutras speak of \"mind only,\" they are making the point that mind is the main thing, and not matter. Although we do of course admit that physical matter exists, this matter is not the one prime creator of sentient beings in the way that mind is. Therefore all these scriptural references are denying that the opposite of the mind, something other than the mind, could be the creator. They are not though saying that there are no outside physical objects at all. 93 The Asian Classics Institute Course V: How Karma Works Reading Six: How Emptiness Allows Karma to Work, According to the Middle-Way School"}

# Root preparation for C05:241–243

Preparation only. No independent proposals or clean baseline exist yet. Withhold
these notes from future proposal readers until their independent drafts freeze.

241 concludes the two senses of mind-only: a negative exclusion of another
creator, and positive emphasis on mind. Preserve repeated principal cause/mind
and matter references, the twelve-link statement, and the distinction between
denying another creator and denying external physical objects. Some English
framing is an expansion; complete phrase depth does not license reversed roles.
The following page93/Reading Six course header is registered apparatus, retained
verbatim in the complete source anchor and not a semantic translation pair.
HGM yan lag bcu gnyis25055 attests a twelve-links term and gtso bo'i rgyu24746
attests principal cause; broader title-like descriptions require local scrutiny.

242 names the work and author. HGM dbu ma spyi don24316 attests Overview of the
Middle Way; mkhas grub bstan pa dar rgyas22065 attests the full English personal
name, including a Master form and dated glossary strings. Distinguish established
name/title convention from added biographical apparatus; do not infer every
character in a glossary string is necessarily a source-licensed alignment span.

243 is a rhetorical argument about Listener/Self-Made-Buddha enemy destroyers,
pervasive afflictions, the path's opposed mode of apprehension and understanding
absence of inherent nature. Keep interlocutor and logical roles intact. HGM
nyan rang5611, dgra bcom pa2451 and phra rgyas24116 provide positive whole-unit
support, and 'dzin stangs15920 attests holds its object. Check complete immediate
parent relations before using any members. Repeated negative conditions and
English questions need exact local correspondence, not a conjunction template.

Full six-field original source/context snapshots and scoped prefix-query rows
are saved. The lexical preparation preserves complete original records. These
are evidence leads only, with no semantic approval or erratum determination.

These references convey yet another meaning of the word \"only\" in the expression \"mind-only.\" Here the point is that mind is the main thing. The former scriptural references are putting their point in a negative way, and the latter references are putting their point in a positive way. Thus we can see that it's the mind which is the one single principal cause that sets all life into motion. The principal cause is not something other than the mind. Therefore when the sutras speak of \"mind only,\" they are making the point that mind is the main thing, and not matter. Although we do of course admit that physical matter exists, this matter is not the one prime creator of sentient beings in the way that mind is. Therefore all these scriptural references are denying that the opposite of the mind, something other than the mind, could be the creator.
